1 A bill to be entitled
2 An act relating to industries and professional
3 activities; amending s. 20.165, F.S.; renaming,

338 Be It Enacted by the Legislature of the State of Florida:
339
340 Section 1. Section 20.165, Florida Statutes, is amended to
341 read:
342 20.165 Department of Business and Professional
343 Regulation.—There is created a Department of Business and
344 Professional Regulation.
345 (1) The head of the Department of Business and
346 Professional Regulation is the Secretary of Business and
347 Professional Regulation. The secretary shall be appointed by the
348 Governor, subject to confirmation by the Senate. The secretary
349 shall serve at the pleasure of the Governor.
350 (2) The following divisions of the Department of Business
Page 14 of 449
CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions.
hb607-00

F L O R I D A H O U S E O F R E P R E S E N T A T I V E S
HB 607 2026
351 and Professional Regulation are established:
352 (a) Division of Administration.
353 (b) Division of Alcoholic Beverages and Tobacco.
354 (c) Division of Certified Public Accounting.
355 1. The director of the division shall be appointed by the
356 secretary of the department, subject to approval by a majority
357 of the Board of Accountancy.
358 2. Effective at the termination of any lease in effect on
359 December 31, 2025, the offices of the division shall be located
360 in Tallahassee Gainesville. Such a lease may not be extended or
361 renewed.
362 (d) Division of Drugs, Devices, and Cosmetics.
363 (e) Division of Florida Condominiums, Timeshares, and
364 Mobile Homes.
365 (f) Division of Hotels and Restaurants.
366 (g) Division of Professions.
367 (h) Division of Real Estate.
368 1. The director of the division shall be appointed by the
369 secretary of the department, subject to approval by a majority
370 of the Florida Real Estate Commission.
371 2. Effective at the termination of any lease in effect on
372 December 31, 2025, the offices of the division shall be located
373 in Tallahassee Orlando. Such a lease may not be extended or
374 renewed.
375 (i) Division of Regulation.

376 (j) Division of Technology.
377 (k) Division of Service Operations.

379 division established within this section. Each division director
380 shall directly administer the division and shall be responsible
381 to the secretary. The secretary may appoint deputy and assistant
382 secretaries as necessary to aid the secretary in fulfilling the
383 secretary's statutory obligations.

426 1. Florida Real Estate Appraisal Board, created under part

430 (c) The following board is established within the Division
431 of Certified Public Accounting: Board of Accountancy, created

433 (5) The members of each board established pursuant to
434 subsection (4) shall be appointed by the Governor, subject to
435 confirmation by the Senate. Consumer members on the board shall
436 be appointed pursuant to subsection (6). Members shall be
437 appointed for 4-year terms, and such terms shall expire on
438 October 31. However, a term of less than 4 years may be utilized
439 to ensure that:
440 (a) No more than two members' terms expire during the same
441 calendar year for boards consisting of seven or eight members.
442 (b) No more than 3 members' terms expire during the same
443 calendar year for boards consisting of 9 to 12 members.
444 (c) No more than 5 members' terms expire during the same
445 calendar year for boards consisting of 13 or more members.
446
447 A member whose term has expired shall continue to serve on the
448 board until such time as a replacement is appointed. A vacancy
449 on the board shall be filled for the unexpired portion of the
450 term in the same manner as the original appointment. No member
Page 18 of 449
CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions.
hb607-00

F L O R I D A H O U S E O F R E P R E S E N T A T I V E S
HB 607 2026
451 may serve for more than the remaining portion of a previous
452 member's unexpired term, plus two consecutive 4-year terms of
453 the member's own appointment thereafter.
454 (6) Each board with five or more members shall have at
455 least two consumer members who are not, and have never been,
456 members or practitioners of the profession regulated by such
457 board or of any closely related profession. Each board with
458 fewer than five members shall have at least one consumer member
459 who is not, and has never been, a member or practitioner of the
460 profession regulated by such board or of any closely related
461 profession.
462 (7) No board, with the exception of joint
463 coordinatorships, shall be transferred from its present location
464 unless authorized by the Legislature in the General
465 Appropriations Act.

795 Statutes, is amended to read:
796 310.0015 Piloting regulation; general provisions.—
797 (3) The rate-setting process, the issuance of licenses
798 only in numbers deemed necessary or prudent by the department
799 board, and other aspects of the economic regulation of piloting
800 established in this chapter are intended to protect the public
Page 32 of 449
CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions.
hb607-00

F L O R I D A H O U S E O F R E P R E S E N T A T I V E S
HB 607 2026
801 from the adverse effects of unrestricted competition which would
802 result from an unlimited number of licensed pilots being allowed
803 to market their services on the basis of lower prices rather
804 than safety concerns. This system of regulation benefits and
805 protects the public interest by maximizing safety, avoiding
806 uneconomic duplication of capital expenses and facilities, and
807 enhancing state regulatory oversight. The system seeks to
808 provide pilots with reasonable revenues, taking into
809 consideration the normal uncertainties of vessel traffic and
810 port usage, sufficient to maintain reliable, stable piloting
811 operations. Pilots have certain restrictions and obligations
812 under this system, including, but not limited to, the following:
813 (a) Pilots may not refuse to provide piloting services to
814 any person or entity that may lawfully request such services,
815 except for justifiable concerns relating to safety, or, in the
816 case of a vessel planning a departure, for nonpayment of
817 pilotage.
818 (b) Pilots may not unilaterally determine the pilotage
819 rates they charge. Such pilotage rates shall instead be
820 determined by the Pilotage Rate Review Committee, in the public
821 interest, as set forth in s. 310.151.
822 (c) Pilots shall maintain or secure adequate pilot boats,
823 office facilities and equipment, dispatch systems, communication
824 equipment and other facilities, and equipment and support
825 services necessary for a modern, dependable piloting operation.

2361 (2) When the department board finds any person guilty of
2362 any of the grounds set forth in subsection (1), it may enter an
2363 order imposing one or more of the following penalties:
2364 (a) Denial of an application for certification.
2365 (b) Permanent revocation.
2366 (c) Suspension of a certificate.
2367 (d) Imposition of an administrative fine not to exceed
2368 $5,000 for each separate offense. Such fine must be rationally
2369 related to the gravity of the violation.
2370 (e) Issuance of a reprimand.
2371 (f) Placement of the certificateholder on probation for a
2372 period of time and subject to such conditions as the department
2373 board may impose, including alteration of performance level.
2374 (g) Satisfactory completion of continuing education.
2375 (h) Issuance of a citation.

7457 489.523 Emergency registration upon death of contractor.—
7458 If an incomplete contract exists at the time of death of a

7460 though not certified. The person shall notify the department
7461 appropriate board, within 30 days after the death of the
7462 contractor, of his or her name and address, knowledge of the

7468 department board, an incomplete contract may be one which has

7470 or her death, or on which he or she was the low bidder and the
7471 contract is subsequently awarded to him or her, regardless of
7472 whether any actual work has commenced under the contract before

10831 barbershop. However, a barber may shampoo, cut, or arrange hair
10832 in a location other than a licensed registered barbershop
10833 without such arrangements.
10834 (3) Any person who holds a valid barber's license in any
10835 state or who is authorized to practice barbering in any country,
10836 territory, or jurisdiction of the United States may perform
10837 barber services in a location other than a licensed registered
10838 barbershop when such services are performed in connection with
10839 the motion picture, fashion photography, theatrical, or
10840 television industry; a manufacturer trade show demonstration; or
10841 an educational seminar.
10842 (4) Pursuant to rules adopted by the department, the
10843 practice of barbering may be performed in a location other than
10844 a licensed barbershop when performed in connection with a
10845 special event and by a person who holds the proper license.
